Two things the gate caught on the way: the fixture's global capture listener swallows Escape, so the panel claims Escape for the open menu the same way it already did for the dialog; and Chromium restores focus to the (now hidden) menu item that opened the centre, so the dialog's close handler returns focus to the launcher (immediately and once more after a 0 ms task). Unit suite 3981 tests, 3978 pass under Node 24 (the 3 known worldOverlayAllocation failures unchanged). New unit test `src/auth/ux.test.mjs` (strength, initials, Caps Lock, CSS placement, SK/EN key parity and formal address). **Published 2026-09-26** on the owner's „áno, nastav, publikuj": `.env` on the server got `AUTH_ORIGINS=https://oko.uhrin.digital`, `AUTH_TRUST_CLOUDFLARE_PROXY=true` (the tunnel is the only public path, so per-IP limits use `CF-Connecting-IP`, honoured only on a loopback socket) and `AUTH_PUBLIC_URL`; the dev server (backend) was restarted, the build came from a clean worktree of `969f0bd` (Vite now emits `globe-*.js` + `account-*.js` instead of `index-*.js`), `scripts/oko-publish.ps1 -SkipBuild` restarted the static task. Verified through the tunnel: the launcher sits in the globe-actions group as a guest; `/account.html` answers `Cache-Control: no-store`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`; `/api/auth/session` 200 with `user: null` and honest capabilities (mail not configured → no verification/reset); `/api/auth/csrf` 200 from the public origin; a `register` with a foreign `Origin` gets 403.
- **Front map: soft combat band, rear settlement dots gone at scene zoom** (2026-09-26, owner on the public Pokrovsk scene: „tie zelené pruhy sprav lepšie a mestá bodky sú veľmi rušivé"). **Band:** the Wikipedia-derived combat band was drawn as amber diagonal hatching on the 0.05° raster; over green terrain it read as yellow-green stair-stepped stripes. `paintControlCanvas` gained `band: 'hatch' | 'soft'` (+ `bandSoft`): the default style now paints the contested cells as one translucent amber ribbon (fill at half the hatch alpha, 1 px per cell into an offscreen canvas, drawn scaled with a `blur(bandSoft·scale/4)` — the same trick the KARTA fills use), KARTA keeps its hatching, and a call without the option still hatches, so the band-width test is unchanged. The timeline legend swatch for „zóna bojov" is a soft radial fill instead of stripes. Second round the same day (owner: „ako že sa nezmenili, je to hrôza" — the soft ribbon over the mirror's occupied fill still read as yellow smears along the whole front): while DeepState draws, from any source, the whole Wikipedia raster is hidden (`setZonesVisible(!dsDraws)` in the timeline; before, the mirror case kept the band because the mirror has no grey zone) and the legend hides the RU-fill and band swatches (`ctlBox.is-nozones`). The front is then the occupied fill + the derived contact line; the soft ribbon remains for the no-DeepState case. **Dots (1):** the settlement-control layer put all 1 150 Wikipedia points on screen at once at the front-scene altitude (~160 km). `controlPointRelevance(p, raster)` (pure, `ukraineControlLayer.js`) now tiers them: contested/mixed points, infrastructure and larger towns (size ≥ 16) are „front" and always drawn (160 on the 13 Aug snapshot; state exposes `frontPoints`); UA/RU settlements within 2 raster cells (≈ 10 km) of the band are „near" (`DistanceDisplayCondition` settlements 120 km, rural 70 km); the rest are „rear" (90 / 55 km); every limited point fades in from 60 % of its distance (`controlPointFarM`). The raster is therefore computed before the points. A first cut kept the 414 near-band points visible at scene zoom — the owner's „je to hrôza" said that was still a wall of dots. **Dots (2):** most of the remaining white dots were the OSM base map's villages — `PLACE_POINT_FAR_M.village` was 230 km (points) with labels only to 160 km, so the Pokrovsk scene showed a 1 800-village cohort as unlabeled 3.5 px dots. Both are 130 km now (`ukraineBase.js`), and village points fade in from 65 % of that distance (`VILLAGE_POINT_FADE_FROM`) instead of popping. Towns and cities are unchanged. Verified on the dev server (ion fallback tiles) on the Pokrovsk scene: rear half of the view carries only labelled towns, the amber ribbon follows the front, the DeepState fill and contact line are untouched. New test `src/data/ukraineControlClutter.test.mjs` (soft vs hatch, relevance, CSS/legend); existing control and base tests unchanged and green. Suite 3984 tests, 3981 pass under Node 24 (the 3 known worldOverlayAllocation failures unchanged).
- **Front map, round 3: glowing contact line and a derived frontline belt** (2026-09-26, owner after round 2 hid the Wikipedia raster: „no teraz tam nevidno vôbec nič na frontovej línii"). With DeepState from the mirror (occupied area only, no grey zone) and the 44-day-old Wikipedia band gone, the front was a 4 px line and a faint fill. **Belt:** `frontZoneRaster(paths, index, { landRings })` in `src/data/ukraineContactLine.js` (pure) derives it from TODAY's contact line: a 0.01° grid (≈ 1.1 × 0.75 km) around the line, distance to the nearest line segment stamped per segment, cells kept when closer than `FRONT_ZONE_KM` = 6, outside Russian control and on Ukrainian land, intensity (1 − d/R)^1.6. Occupied territory and land are tested with exact scanline masks (`ringMask`, even-odd per ring) — a first version used the nearest segment's side and leaked 38–51 cells into occupied territory at the teeth of the outline; the mask gives 0 and runs in ≈ 70 ms on the 26 Sep mirror file (561 × 474 cells, 7 577 belt cells), once per snapshot and only when the style draws it. `paintZoneCanvas` puts it on a canvas (colour `#ff7a3d`, max alpha 0.42) shown as a clamped rectangle. Hover: „prifrontové pásmo: do 6 km od dnešnej línie kontaktu na ukrajinskej strane — odvodená geometria, nie mapa bojov". **Glow:** under the crisp red line a `PolylineGlowMaterialProperty` line (18 px, alpha 0.6, glowPower 0.14, zIndex 9) — the front reads at the scene altitude (~160 km) and up close. Both only in the default style; KARTA stays thin (its legend hides the belt swatch). Timeline legend: new swatch „prifrontové pásmo (≤ 6 km od línie, odvodené)". Verified on the dev server on the Pokrovsk scene at 160 km and at 43 km (the owner's view): the red glowing line and the orange belt on the Ukrainian side are the first thing the eye finds; no console errors. New test `src/data/ukraineFrontZone.test.mjs`. Suite 3988 tests, 3985 pass under Node 24 (the 3 known worldOverlayAllocation failures unchanged).
- **Front map, round 4: hatched occupied side, exactly registered ground rasters** (2026-09-26, owner pointing at the occupied salient Karpivka–Ridkodub–Nove–Zelena Dolyna north of Lyman: „tú hluché miesto by si mohol vyplniť šrafovaním"). The salient has been occupied for at least six months (mirror files 26 Mar – 26 Sep all agree), so it is not a „recent advance" — it just read as bare terrain under the 0.28 occupied fill. **Hatch:** `frontZoneRaster` now also returns `ruValues` — occupied cells within `FRONT_ZONE_RU_KM` = 8 km of the contact line, full to 4 km, then fading — and the default style draws them as red 45° screen-space hatching (`zoneRu`), so salients and pockets stand out while deep occupied territory (Donetsk, Crimea) stays a plain fill. KARTA gets neither. Legend swatch „okupovaná strana pri línii (≤ 8 km, šrafa)", hover text says it is derived geometry (the tip picks UA/RU by the polygons under the cursor, because both sides are one rectangle). **Registration bug found on the way:** both ground rasters were drawn with `ImageMaterialProperty` on one large clamped rectangle (the belt ≈ 5.7° × 4.8°, the Wikipedia zones 18° × 8°); Cesium computes texture coordinates for such ground primitives with a spherical approximation, and the belt sat kilometres off the polygons (orange belt inside the salient, hatch north of the line, while the raster itself had 0 mismatches against point-in-polygon). New materials in `src/data/screenPatternMaterials.js`: `OkoFrontZone` (texture R = Ukrainian-side belt, G = occupied-side hatch mask) and `OkoGeoImage` (plain rgba, now used for the Wikipedia zone raster) compute each fragment's geodetic lon/lat themselves — the view ray from `positionToEyeEC` intersected with the WGS84 ellipsoid, then `atan(z, p·(1 − e²))` — and sample the texture by `rect` (radians). Two traps, both verified with a debug checkerboard and point markers in the pane: `positionToEyeEC` is not divided by `w` in the classification shader, so it can only be used as a direction (as a position the whole rectangle came out one colour); and `czm_rayEllipsoidIntersectionInterval` takes the ray and the ellipsoid centre in EYE coordinates (origin 0, centre `czm_view[3].xyz`) — with world coordinates the grid came out scaled by ~1.3. Terrain height is ignored (≈ 70 m at the scene pitch). Verified top-down and at the owner's oblique view: hatch ends exactly at the red line, the orange belt stays on the Ukrainian side; the Wikipedia raster (DeepState off) now has the contested dots inside the amber band. Tests extended in `src/data/ukraineFrontZone.test.mjs`. Suite 3990 tests, 3987 pass under Node 24 (the 3 known worldOverlayAllocation failures unchanged).
- **Preloader: a swirl of OKO text around the eye, like the midjourney.com landing** (2026-09-26/27, owner: „preloader v štýle úvodnej stránky midjourney.com… stredové oko bude obtekané… to oko v strede nemeň ani text… ja to chcem ako Midjourney"). `public/preloaderFlow.js` is a small import-free module loaded in `index.html` BEFORE `main.js` from public/ — Vite would otherwise merge every module script of index.html into the 2.3 MB app bundle — so it draws while the big bundle still downloads; it inserts `canvas#loader-flow` under `.loader-content` (style.css: content `position: relative; z-index: 1`, canvas absolute) — the eye, OKO, status and credit are untouched. **Midjourney technique** (studied on their page — timed screenshots 0–21 s and their client code): a fixed monospace grid in one colour; a block of left-aligned prompt lines of varying length is read through a swirl (cell in −1…1 normalised to width/height, rotated by clock × 0.1 / max(0.1, r)), so the centre winds faster and the line starts/ends form elliptical spiral arms; then a CRT pass (curvature eases in over 3 s, chroma shift decays, strong scanlines, vignette, exposure 1 − e^(−2.5x)). **OKO:** the block is „/oko …" lines from OKO layers (flights, vessels, satellites, gauges, radar, gas, front; status ŽIVÉ, rarely amber ODHAD/OMEŠKANÉ), the swirl turns around the eye (`.loader-logo` centre), a crisp rounded-rectangle clear zone hugs `.loader-content` (no fade — the owner rejected fading and a physical flow-around-a-cylinder variant with empty wedges), each `.loader-status` change speeds the swirl clock up, intro = random-glyph „decode" + blur that sharpens (owner liked it), on hide the swirl speeds up during the 0.8 s fade and the GL context is lost. **Cost** (owner: „mimoriadne náročné na hardvér" for an earlier 3-pass 60 fps version): cells are computed on the GPU into a small grid texture, the screen is ONE pass with one cell + one atlas read per pixel (multi-tap only during the intro), 30 fps, dpr ≤ 1.5 — measured 0.03 ms/frame at 1080p and 0.16 ms at 2560×1440@2 on a GTX 1080 Ti (Midjourney re-rasterises text with fillText and uploads the whole frame every frame at 60 fps). `?preloader=demo` holds the preloader over the finished app (Esc/click ends) and pauses the Cesium render loop underneath (it would render Google 3D tiles at 60 fps for nobody — also quota); `main.js` `syncVisibilitySuspension` re-enables the loop on tab return, so the pause is re-applied every frame and restored to `!document.hidden` afterwards. Reduced motion (the owner's Windows reports it) → the swirl turns at half speed (a still frame looked „zamrznuté", 2026-09-27); no WebGL2 → the old static preloader. Tests in `src/preloaderFlow.test.mjs`.
- **KARTA: right panel rail docked at the top of the left column** (2026-09-26, the owner circled the rail beside the overview inset and drew an arrow to the top of the left column: „tot musí zmiznúť a dať do tam kde som dal šípku"; interim until the panel tidy-up; supersedes the placement below). `src/kartaRailDock.js` moves the whole `#right-context-rail` (Zobrazenie / Kamery / Kontext keep their `#right-context-rail > …` styles) in as the first child of `#left-panel-stack` while the KARTA frame is visible and puts it back exactly where it was when the frame hides or the overlay is destroyed (`ukraineKartaOverlay` `applyVisibility` / `destroy`). Position changes inline (creditAttribution.test guards the rail geometry in CSS); lane order via `#left-panel-stack > .oko-rail-docked { order: 0 }`. Expanded rail panels are collapsed for the frame's duration through `ui.setPanelCollapsed(id, true, { persist: false, syncShare: false })` (main.js passes it; expanded Zobrazenie is ~790 px and left DÁTOVÉ VRSTVY 0 px) and re-expanded on exit; expanded while docked they cap at `min(46vh, 420px)` and scroll inside. `_syncRightPanelAdaptiveLayout` returns early with `data-layout-mode="docked"` (no corridor, no exclusive hiding). The old `body.oko-karta-frame #right-context-rail { right: 214px; }` is gone; the legend's `data-rail-bound` / `bottomBound` stay in the resolver. Also in the frame: an expanded left panel squeezed to 0 px in the low corridor (open timeline) painted its header over the KONFLIKTY zone title — `body.oko-karta-frame #left-panel-stack > [data-panel-id]:not(.collapsed) { min-height: min(30vh, 200px); }`, the lane scrolls. Nothing moves on mobile (`body.oko-mobile`). Verified on localhost at 1525×808: pills at 185 / 243 / 301 px, DÁTOVÉ VRSTVY 359–559 px, the right side holds only tools, inset and legend; KARTA off → rail back on the right, Zobrazenie expanded again. Tests in `src/ukraineKartaLayout.test.mjs`. Full suite (Node 22 on PATH): 4093 tests, 4092 pass; the one failure, `src/auth/server/migration.test.mjs`, needs `node:sqlite` `backup` (Node 24) and passes 6/6 under Node 24.
- **Right panel rail moved off the KARTA legend** (2026-09-26, owner circled the rail — Zobrazenie / Kamery / Kontext — lying across the legend: „zakrúžkované treba presunúť a neskôr sa bude robiť poriadok s týmto"; an interim placement until the panels are tidied). Cause: `resolveHudRailLayout` splits obstacles at the viewport midpoint and ignores one that straddles it — the bottom-right legend (≈ 360–590 px on a 808 px screen) did exactly that. **Now:** in the KARTA frame the rail stands left of the overview inset (`body.oko-karta-frame #right-context-rail { right: 214px; }` = inset right 36 + width 168 + gap 10; only `right` — top/height stay with the layout engine, as creditAttribution.test requires), starts at the KARTA tools row instead of the left lane top (ui.js `alignedTop` takes `.oko-karta-tools` top when the frame is visible), and ends above the legend: the legend carries `data-rail-bound="bottom"`, ui.js passes `bottomBound` with the obstacle and the resolver bounds the rail's bottom by it even across the midpoint — only when at least `minBoundHeight` (150 px) remains above, otherwise the old behaviour (overlap) wins so the panels never vanish. The rail's MutationObserver also watches `<html>` style, because a timeline collapse moves the legend through `--oko-ukr-tl-lift` without resizing it. Verified on localhost at 1525×808 with the collapsed timeline: rail 130–349 px, legend from 359 px, Zobrazenie scrolls inside. Test in `src/ukraineKartaLayout.test.mjs`. Suite under Node 24: 4 109 tests, 4 106 pass; the three failures are the known timing-sensitive frame-budget cases in worldOverlayAllocation.test.mjs.
- **KARTA polish after the owner's Rybar review: paired arrows, crisp 7-day change areas, news cards at the frame edge** (2026-09-26, owner with an annotated screenshot: „šípka veľká, radšej dve menšie", „tá modrá hmlistá to nie je Rybar" → „hmlovinu prerob na jasnejšiu, svetlejšiu s jasnými vymedzeniami približnými", „mal som tam aj kartičky s news a tie zmizli", „najprv to rob na localhost a potom publikuj"; memory note „Ukrajina = štýl Rybar"). **Arrows:** every LOD draws a PAIR of smaller arrows (`attackArrowPair`: the path shifted ±`pairOffsetKm` perpendicular at the line and ±offset × `ARROW_PAIR_CONVERGE` (0.35) at the tip, same bend side so they stay parallel); `ARROW_LODS` near scale 0.7 / offset 1.1 km, far scale 1.15 / 9–15 km / offset 2 km (was one arrow at scale 2). **7-day change in KARTA:** the blurred cell raster („hmla") became vector areas: `maskToGridRings` traces the cell mask along cell edges (outer rings and holes, saddles split consistently), `smoothRing` (Chaikin ×2) rounds the stairs, `changeAreaPolygons` returns `{ lost, gained }` [lon,lat] polygons with holes and drops areas under 2 cells; `DEEPSTATE_STYLES.karta.change = { mode: 'vector', lost #8fd3ff 0.45 + edge #e6f6ff, gained #ff6b78 0.4 + edge #ffd6da, 2 px }` — boundaries are clear but approximate (0.01° cells), the legend and hover still say „odvodené"; the default style keeps the raster. **News cards:** the closer top-down KARTA framing held 1 of 340 events (news are often geocoded only to an oblast, e.g. „Kharkiv Oblast" ~90 km from Lyman). Cards now also come from the view rectangle grown by `NEAR_VIEW_FRACTION` (0.6) on each side (only at card zoom); in-view events keep priority and the „v zábere" count stays strict. An off-screen anchor is clamped to the viewport (`clampAnchorToViewport`, inset 26 px) and nudged toward the centre out of the panels with room for the card (`nudgeEdgeAnchor`, pad 60×72 px); the pin becomes an arrow rotated toward the real place (`.oko-ukr-pin.is-edge`), the card is dashed and slightly dimmer; a card that still does not fit is hidden together with its pin. Verified on localhost before publishing (narrow pane: Kharkiv, Donetsk, Dobropillia at the edge; 1440×900: two). Tests: `src/data/ukraineRybarFixes.test.mjs`, pair test in `ukraineAttackArrows.test.mjs`. Suite under Node 24: 4 108 tests, 4 105 pass; the three failures are the known timing-sensitive frame-budget cases.
- **Real DeepState grey zone from a mirror (SmartFinn/wararchive-website), drawn in KARTA like Rybar's combat territory** (2026-09-26, owner: „dokonči všetko a použi sivú zónu s mirrorov!"). cyterat and its fork lazar-bit keep only the occupied multipolygon (lazar-bit's `script.py` filters to „CADR and CALR / Occupied / Occupied Crimea"); a GitHub search of repos that fetch `deepstatemap.live/api/history` found `SmartFinn/wararchive-website`, whose `data/deepstate.geojson` is the API `map` minus points and a few far territories — 31 „Unknown status" (grey) polygons, 25 occupied, 38 liberated, 15 liberated recently, Crimea/ORDLO/Tuzla on 2026-09-26; the file is committed only when it changes, 371 versions since 2025-06-29. **Server:** `src/data/deepstateFullMirror.js` — `createDeepStateFullMirror().lookup(day)` takes the last commit ≤ end of the day (UTC) from the file's commit list (GitHub API, unauthenticated; memory 3 h + `.gev-cache/deepstate-full/commits.json`, only page 1 refreshed), downloads the file by SHA from raw.githubusercontent.com (immutable, cached forever), 15 min pause after 403/429/5xx/timeout, 12 MB cap; `deepstateSnapshotFromFullMirror` runs the API model (`deepstateSnapshotFromApi`) and marks it `source: 'mirror'`, `mirror: 'wararchive'`, time = commit time (approximate), `fallbackDays` = days between commit and requested day. `ukraineEventsProxy` tries it first when vite.config.js passes `deepstateFullMirror` (env `UKRAINE_DEEPSTATE_FULL_MIRROR=off` disables), and falls back to cyterat when the file is older than `FULL_MIRROR_MAX_AGE_DAYS` (7) or the mirror fails; licence block and note say „not open data", the mirror declares no licence. Measured: `?at=2026-09-26` → file of 25.9., grey 1 666 km². **Client:** a real grey zone replaces the derived band (`hasGrey` in rebuild, both styles); in KARTA the grey zone is the orange Rybar hatch (`DEEPSTATE_STYLES.karta.greyCss #f0922e`, hatch 0.92/0.26/6/0.4 — the 2026-09-20 pin „KARTA grey must not change" was updated on the owner's explicit request); `sideAt` treats a mirror with grey like the archive (outside polygons = UA, grey = contested); liberated areas keep their light fill but lose their outlines in KARTA (`noLiberatedOutline` — lines across the whole Kharkiv region were clutter; the 2026-09-24 „KARTA solid colours" pin still holds). Legend: „zóna bojov (sivá)" in orange hatch when the snapshot has grey, „oslobodené (DeepState)" when liberated areas are drawn, the derived „contested" item only for the partial mirror; timeline classes `is-mirror` (partial) / `is-full-mirror` / `has-grey` hide the swatches the map does not draw; mirror repo names come from `deepstateMirrorRepo('wararchive')`. **Oblast border in KARTA:** solid white 2.2 px (`OBLAST_KARTA_STYLE`) instead of the faint dashed line. Fortifications stay out: ISW forbids use without written consent, Brady Africk's KML has no licence and OSM has practically none (plan: „Opevnenia: nikdy bez súhlasu"). Tests: `src/data/deepstateFullMirror.test.mjs`. Suite under Node 24: 4 102 tests, 4 099 pass; the three failures are the known timing-sensitive frame-budget cases in worldOverlayAllocation.test.mjs.
- **KARTA closer to the Rybar sample: two-colour map, bigger labels, closer framing** (2026-09-26, owner asked „podľa teba je to ako Rybar?", got the assessment „kostrou áno, vzhľadom na polovicu" with three cheap steps, answered „sprav"). **Two-colour map:** `DEEPSTATE_STYLES.karta` fills occupied areas in dark burgundy (`#8e2330` at 0.64, 2014 areas 0.52, outline `#3d0a0e`) instead of salmon red, and tints the Ukrainian side dark blue (`uaTint #2f6aa3` at 0.3): `paintUaTintCanvas` fills `UKRAINE_LAND_RINGS` on a 150 px/° canvas, cuts out (destination-out) every Russian-held and grey polygon with even-odd holes and the KARTA combat band canvas, and one ground rectangle `:ua-tint` draws it through `OkoGeoImage` (exact registration, anti-aliased vector edges instead of cell stairs); no properties on purpose, so no tooltip hangs over the whole country. Measured on the pane: the Ukrainian side went from rgb(27,40,54) to rgb(34,63,91). Legend item „ukrajinská kontrola (mimo okupovaného a zóny bojov, odvodené)", timeline swatch `is-ds-ua` only in KARTA, burgundy swatch for occupied in KARTA. **Labels:** `UKRAINE_BASE_STYLES.karta` now scales fonts ×1.15 in Inter 600 (loaded in index.html up to 600) with white settlement names (`placeLabelCss`); lines and points stay fine (owner's 2026-09-20 taste). GS-report settlement labels use the same face in KARTA (`placeLabelFont`). The 2026-09-20 pin in `ukraineBaseLayer.test.mjs` („13 px × 0,9") was changed to the newly requested size (15 px, Inter 600) — the only older assertion touched. **Framing:** `frontSceneFraming(rect, { karta: true })` — pitch −80° and `spanDeg × 72 km` height (Lyman 97 km instead of 159 km at −64°, slant distance to the centre ≈ 99 km instead of 177 km), camera offset so the view axis hits the frame centre; main.js passes `karta: !scene.overview`. The −64° rule existed because of the CPU cost of a horizon full of Google 3D tiles, which KARTA does not load; the whole-front overview and mideast theatres keep their framing. The round vignette is the HUD scope setting (`sc`) and was left alone. Tests: `src/data/ukraineKartaRybarLook.test.mjs`. Suite under Node 24: 4 096 tests, 4 093 pass; the three failures are the known timing-sensitive frame-budget cases in worldOverlayAllocation.test.mjs.
- **KARTA settlements as hexagons by side, road shields by class, the Ukrainian side from the mirror, bigger arrows from afar** (2026-09-26, item 4 of the post-tidy proposal, owner: „pokračuj", Rybar sample). **Hexagons:** in KARTA a settlement whose side is known is drawn as a pointy-top hexagon in the side colour (`SIDE_PIN_COLORS`, dark rim, light centre dot), size by class `HEX_PIN_PX` (city 17 / town 13 / village 10 px); every place entity carries a hidden billboard with the point's display condition and village fade, `applySide` swaps point ↔ billboard and moves the label past the hexagon; outside KARTA or with an unknown side it stays the small point. The image is a data URL from `defaultHexPinImage` (one texture-atlas entry per colour — a canvas would be added to the atlas once per billboard); measured on `?front=lyman`: 3 030 of 3 242 places are hexagons, 3 distinct images. Legend swatches `pin-ua`/`pin-ru` became `glyph: 'hex'` (CSS `.glyph-hex`, drawn in the share export too). **Ukrainian side from the mirror:** the mirror has only occupied polygons, so settlements outside them used to have no side (grey dots). `sideAt` now returns 'ua' for mirror snapshots when the point is on Ukrainian land (`UKRAINE_LAND_RINGS`) and outside the derived band (`frontZoneRaster(…, { radiusKm: FRONT_ZONE_KM })`, 6 km from today's line); inside the band — the derived stand-in for the grey zone — the side stays unknown; results cached per snapshot. Russia, Belarus, Slovakia etc. stay unknown; archive (API) snapshots keep their old behaviour. **Road shields:** `roadShieldFarM(ref)` — M/H/E 380 km, P 200 km, T/O 110 km, local (C…, other) 50 km, so the direction view (≈ 180 km) shows only the main roads (measured: 290 + 21 + 214 of 1 583 shields reach that far). **Arrows:** `ARROW_LODS` — near (< 90 km camera distance) the original arrow, far (90–460 km) a second polygon at twice the scale and 11–18 km long, because at the direction view (1 km ≈ 2–5 px) the near arrow was a 10–30 px dash. Tests: `src/data/ukraineHexPins.test.mjs`, `ukraineAttackArrows.test.mjs`. Suite under Node 24: 4 092 tests, 4 089 pass; the three failures are the known timing-sensitive frame-budget cases in worldOverlayAllocation.test.mjs.
- **Attack-direction arrows, a crisp front edge and a Rybar-like grey zone in KARTA** (2026-09-26, owner with the Rybar sample again: „a šípky smerov útoku?", „frontová línia ostré hrany a nie je vidno ani šedá zóna", „so sivou zónou ako Rybar!"). **Arrows:** the DeepState API carries attack directions, the mirror does not and there is no consent — so the arrow is DERIVED from two things OKO has: the settlement at which the General Staff daily report places assaults (the bolt) and today's DeepState contact line. `ukraineAttackArrows.js` (pure): `attackArrowPath(from, to)` starts `ARROW_BACK_KM` (3) behind the nearest line point inside occupied territory, ends `ARROW_HEAD_GAP_KM` (0.6) short of the settlement, clips at `ARROW_MAX_KM` (12), skips settlements within `ARROW_MIN_KM` (0.8) of the line, bends as a quadratic Bézier whose side comes from the name hash; width by the direction's attack count (5/6.5/8 px). The report layer (`drawArrows`) draws each as two clamped `PolylineArrow` polylines (dark outline zIndex 12 under the red `#ff3b30` arrow zIndex 13, DDC ≤ 460 km), only for settlements on the Ukrainian side per DeepState (`sideAt`), only in the KARTA style, and redraws them on every new line (`reanchor`). The DeepState layer gained `nearestContactPoint(lon, lat, { reportDay })`; main.js passes `contactPoint`/`sideAt` to the report layer. Hovering an arrow opens the settlement card with an extra line „šípka vedie od dnešnej línie kontaktu DeepState k tomuto sídlu — odvodená geometria, nie mapa GŠ"; KARTA legend item „smer útoku (hlásenie GŠ, odvodené)" and a swatch in the timeline's GS row. Verified on `?front=lyman`: 21 arrows for 35 named settlements. **Crisp edge:** in KARTA the derived contact line is no longer drawn (`DEEPSTATE_STYLES.karta.contact = null` — it zigzagged inside the band like a saw); the front edge is the polygon outline itself: solid fill `fillCss`/`fillAlpha` (occupied #b8352f at 0.46, 2014 areas 0.36) with a dark 1.6 px outline (`outlineCss` #5e0f0f). The line is still computed (band, arrows, inset, hover). **Grey zone:** the mirror has no grey zone, so the combat band is its derived stand-in and now looks like Rybar's: mostly on the Ukrainian side (`uaKm` 6, `ruKm` 2.5), denser and more visible hatch (line 0.92, fill 0.26, spacing 6). Tests: `src/data/ukraineAttackArrows.test.mjs`; own pins in ukraineContactLine.test and ukraineFrontZone.test moved to the new design. Suite under Node 24: 4 086 tests, 4 083 pass; the three failures are the known timing-sensitive frame-budget cases in worldOverlayAllocation.test.mjs (they fail in isolation too and predate this work).
- **Seven-day front change from the DeepState mirror, and settlement pins that follow DeepState while Wikipedia is stale** (2026-09-26, items 2 and 3 of the post-tidy proposal the owner accepted with „začni"). **Change:** after every DeepState snapshot the layer fetches the mirror file from `CHANGE_DAYS` (7) days earlier through the same `/api/ukraine/events/deepstate?at=` proxy (disk-cached, the proxy may fall back to an older file — the real span is carried as `fromDay`/`days`), and `occupiedChangeRaster` (ukraineContactLine.js) rasterises the Russian-controlled rings of both snapshots on a shared 0.01° grid (scanline `ringMask`, ≤ 4 M cells) and diffs them: cells Russian today but not before = occupied (`ruValues`), the reverse = liberated (`values`); areas are cell sums with cos(lat), rounded to 0.1 km². It is a GEOMETRIC difference of two daily polygons, not a DeepState figure — every label says „odvodené / derived", the timeline line („zmena za 7 d · RU +N km² · UA +M km²") carries a tooltip naming both files. Drawn as one `:change` rectangle over the front band (zIndex 8) with the `OkoFrontZone` material: occupied = dense crimson screen-space hatch with fill, liberated = solid blue (`DEEPSTATE_STYLES.*.change`, KARTA darker); hover resolves the cell under the cursor and otherwise drill-picks what lies beneath, so polygons and the band stay readable. State exposes `change {days, fromDay, toDay, gainedKm2, lostKm2, gainedCells, lostCells}` + `changeLoading`; the KARTA title adds „za 7 dní: RU +N km² · UA +M km²", the KARTA legend and the timeline legend gain the two swatches (only when cells exist). A result computed for another day is discarded; a stale raster is dropped when the snapshot day changes. Verified on `?front=lyman`: file 2026-09-19 → 2026-09-26, RU +10.7 km² (13 cells), UA +33.2 km² (41 cells). **Pins:** `pickPlaceSide` (ukraineBase.js): Wikipedia points (≤ 3 km, they also carry „contested") decide while their snapshot is fresh; once it is stale (`CONTROL_STALE_DAYS`, Wikipedia stood at 44 days) today's DeepState polygons decide and Wikipedia only fills where the polygons are silent. Tests: `src/data/ukraineFrontChange.test.mjs`. Suite under Node 24: 4 030 tests, 4 029 pass; the single failure is again the parallel agent's uncommitted `activeTheatre` change in main.js versus the mapScaleBar assertion — not part of this commit.
- **KARTA frame tidied — islands sit in chrome-free bands and the panel lanes flow around them** (2026-09-26, owner: „začni… Chcem to prehľadné!!!"). Measured in the pane at 1440×900 on `?front=lyman`: the KARTA title lay across the OKO logo (`#title-bar` ends at 89 px) and the left panel lane (from 100 px); the tools and the overview inset lay across the language switch (84–121 px) and the right rail; the legend at bottom-left ran across the Cesium/Google credit line (645 px) and the open timeline (694 px); the scale bar ended under the command dock. **Now:** title top-left BELOW the logo (top 98, left 36 like the logo; state and sources share one wrapping row); tools (top 130) and inset (top 178) top-right BELOW the language switch (right 36 like the style indicator); legend bottom-RIGHT above the open timeline (`bottom: max(96px, --oko-ukr-tl-lift + 4px)` — the floor clears the voice pill), two wrapping columns (one below 1 348 px so it never reaches under the centred dock), with the scale bar anchored into its footer slot `.oko-karta-legend-scale` (mapScaleBar.js, z-index 93 over the island; kept in the clean map too). The islands are lane obstacles: `.oko-karta-title` in `LEFT_STACK_OBSTACLE_SELECTOR`, tools/inset/legend in `RIGHT_STACK_OBSTACLE_SELECTOR`, registered after creation via `observeLeftStackObstacle` and the new `observeRightStackObstacle` (ui.js) — the left lane flows under the title, the rail between the inset and the legend. `body.oko-karta-frame` (toggled with visibility) hides the coordinate corner `.hud-bottom-left` and bounds the left lane to its corridor with `overflow-y: auto`, because the pillar of collapsed pills (~440 px) does not fit below the title on ≤ 900 px viewports and used to overflow onto the credits (z-index never helps, only capacity). The legend deliberately is NOT bottom-left like Rybar's: that column is the panel pillar. Mobile (≤ 900 px): title narrowed beside the tools, legend above the lifted credits and hidden while the timeline is open (the timeline has its own LEGENDA toggle). Verified: no island overlaps chrome at 1440×900, 1525×860 and 375×812. New guard `src/ukraineKartaLayout.test.mjs`. Suite under Node 24: 4 009 tests, 4 008 pass; the single failure is the mapScaleBar assertion on `syncMapFocus` in main.js, which the parallel agent's uncommitted `activeTheatre` work changed — not part of this commit.
- **Front scenes open in KARTA, styled after the owner's Rybar sample** (2026-09-26, owner with a Rybar „Лиманское направление" map: „ja som to chcel takto, aj s malým náhľadom", then „Áno, front vždy v KARTE"). KARTA already had the title, legend, overview map, settlement pins by side, GS-report bolts and road shields (K0–K5); with DeepState from the mirror it lacked the combat zone, and since round 2 its legend still promised „zóna bojov (Wikipédia)" that nothing drew. **Combat band:** `DEEPSTATE_STYLES.karta.combatBand` — an orange (#f0922e) 45° screen-space hatch with a light orange fill between the stripes (the `OkoFrontZone` material gained a `hatchFill` uniform), spanning BOTH sides of today's contact line (3 km toward Ukraine, 5 km into occupied territory; `frontZoneRaster(..., { radiusKm: 3, ruRadiusKm: 5 })` → `combatBandMask` = one full mask). Rasters are cached per (UA km, RU km) per snapshot, so switching styles does not recompute. Legend key `contested` now always appears with the mirror, orange hatch, label „zóna bojov (pás pri línii, odvodený)" (no longer „Wikipédia"); hover „odvodená geometria, nie mapa bojov". **Overview („malý náhľad"):** moved from bottom-right (half hidden behind the timeline) to top-right under the KARTA tools, Ukraine filled blue and the Russian-controlled area in maroon from a coarse DeepState outline (`coarseOccupiedRings`, points thinned to ≥ 0.06°, via the layer's new `occupiedOutline()`), plus the view rectangle and the scene dot; the share-image export draws it top-right too. **Default:** `runFrontScene` switches the map stack to KARTA when a direction opens (URL `?front=`, panel, voice) and remembers the previous stack; it is restored when a chokepoint scene or another conflict opens or the timeline is closed, unless the user changed the stack by hand meanwhile. Verified on `?front=lyman`: stack `karta`, frame visible, band along the whole line, inset with occupied area. Tests added to `src/data/ukraineFrontZone.test.mjs`. Suite 3992 tests, 3989 pass under Node 24 (the 3 known worldOverlayAllocation failures unchanged).

I; domains and legal acts verified the same day against OpenSanctions/EUR-Lex). `src/data/sanctionedMedia.js` gains a Middle East section with three new `basis` tokens named after the regime, and every non-Annex-XV entry carries a `note` with the act: `eu-iran-hr` = Reg. (EU) 359/2011 (Press TV 2022/2231, IRIB 2022/2428 + its channels Al-Alam/HispanTV/Sahar/YJC as owner-listed, Tasnim 2023/1779 — NOT 2025/776 as the survey guessed); `eu-terror-list` = 2001/931/CFSP + Reg. 2580/2001 (IRGC designated 19 Feb 2026 → Sepah News; Fars only PRECAUTIONARY — OFAC says „acting for or on behalf of the IRGC", the EU does not list it, so the entry name says „precautionary"; Hamas whole organisation → Al-Aqsa TV, al-Qassam (also GHRSR), Shehab, PIC; PIJ → Saraya al-Quds; Hizballah Military Wing → Al-Manar/Al-Nour precautionary); `eu-yemen` = Reg. (EU) 1352/2014 (Ansarallah 2022/419 → Al-Masirah, Saba **of Sanaa** `saba.ye` — the Aden agency `sabanew.net` of the recognised government stays unblocked). Test additions: domain uniqueness across entries (the host index is a Map, last would win silently), lower-case, `note` mandatory outside Annex XV/I, positive/negative host lists per region. - **MIDDLE EAST module, stage 2 — KONTROLA SÍDIEL from four Wikipedia modules** (2026-09-26, owner: „pokračuj v blízkom východe"; plan `docs/drafts/blizky-vychod-plan.md` ch. 5/6 stage 2, source survey §A1; legends of all four modules verified 24 Sep 2026 against each module's `containerArgs.caption` (= the rendered template legend) and `/doc`; three survey agents (Ukraine control layer line by line, legends, i18n/test conventions), implementers W (parser), S (server), L (client), C (wiring + docs) and a parallel fix agent applying the review findings). **Core `src/data/wikiControl.js`** — ONE config-driven parser for every Wikipedia „detailed map" Lua module. Config shape: `{ id, titles:[{id,title,since?}], attribution, license, bbox, cellDeg, maxKm, bandKm, contestedKm, sides:[{id,label,css,fill}], colours:[{re,side}] (ORDERED — `darkred` before `red`, `lightslategray` before `gray`), icons (ordered rules over the normalised file name: `match`, `side`|FROM_COLOUR, `kind`, `pressure`, `direction`, `between`|FROM_COLOURS, `skip` for background images ≥ marksize 40, `sizeMax`, `resolve`), contestedCss/mixedCss/noneCss, colocated:'merge', defaultMk, marksizePop, staleDays, i18nPrefix 'mideast.ctl.<id>', alsoModules? }`. Exports `wikiControlPoints/Summary/Codes/Colours/Raster`, `wikiMarkStatus`, `wikiSideText`, `stripLuaComments` (line + block comments, not inside strings/long brackets), `normalizeIconName`, `plainLabel`; codes = side index + 1 in `sides` order, contested = sides.length + 1, none = 0 (reproduces Ukraine's frozen 0/1/2/3); the N-side raster finds the nearest point per side through spatial buckets, band when d2 − d1 ≤ bandKm or a contested/mixed settlement within contestedKm, counts keyed by side; `summary.unmapped` COUNTS unknown icons instead of dropping them, `skipped` counts overlays. **Ukraine became a config** (`UKRAINE_CONTROL_CONFIG`; `src/data/ukraineControl.js` = thin wrappers keeping every export, default and exact output shape) — all Ukraine test files pass WITHOUT edits, including the source-text regexes of `ukraineControlClutter.test.mjs`. **Four module configs** `MIDEAST_CONTROL_MODULES` (`israel-palestine` 0.02° / 12 / 3 / 3 km, stale 14 d, 9 sides; `yemen` 0.05° / 35 / 7 / 7, 30 d; `syria` same grid, 45 d; `lebanon` 0.02° / 12 / 3 / 3, 30 d, `alsoModules: ['israel-palestine']`). Fixtures `src/data/fixtures/wiki-{israel-palestine,yemen,lebanon}-20260924.lua` + `wiki-syria-20260924-head.lua` (trimmed head — the full module is 958 KB; README rows). Measured on the fixtures: IP 1 317 merged points (1 329 active marks, 0 unmapped), Yemen 1 279 (449 red / 418 green town dots), Lebanon 85, Syria head 622 merged (631 active marks, 1 overlay skipped); the IP raster (255×335 cells, ~1 300 points) takes ≈ 27 ms warm through the buckets. **Server:** `scripts/lib/mideastArchive.mjs` (the Ukraine MediaWiki recipe: `rvstart=<day>T23:59:59Z&rvdir=older`, User-Agent `OKO-mideast/0.1 (…; vladouh76@gmail.com)`, 60 s timeout, 6 MB cap enforced WHILE STREAMING chunked bodies (abort + cancel), `now` validated as a function or finite number) writes `.gev-cache/mideast/events/control/<module>/<day>.json` = `{ day, module, revisionAt, revisions:{main:{title,revid,timestamp,url = revision permalink}}, count, summary:{settlements:{<side>…,contested,mixed,none}, infrastructure, unmapped}, points, license, attribution, source }`; today is fresh for 6 h, past days final after 2 days; `controlFor` walks BACK over unreadable files (a truncated snapshot never hides an older valid one). CLI `scripts/build-mideast-events.mjs --all | --control [--module <id>] [--force] | --control-history [--module <id>] [--from 2026-02-28] [--step 7] [--limit N]` — one request at a time, 1.2 s pauses; first real snapshot 2026-09-26: lebanon 85 points, revision 1375965759. `src/data/mideastEventsProxy.js` mounted at `/api/mideast/events` in `vite.config.js`: `GET /control?module=<id>&at=YYYY-MM-DD` → 200 snapshot + `requestedAt/snapshots/first/last`, 400 `bad_module` (with the module list) / `bad_day`, 404 `no_control_snapshot`, 500 `archive_read_failed` when days ≤ at exist but none is readable; `/status` public without error texts; cache 10 min keyed `module:at` (64 keys), global limiter 429, gzip ONLY for an explicit `gzip` token with q > 0 and `Vary: Accept-Encoding` on every compressible 200; archiver = one job per module every 6 h, first after 200 s staggered by 60 s, a `stopped` flag ends ticks after a vite restart (no zombie archiver), `MIDEAST_ARCHIVE=off`. **Client:** `src/data/mideastControlClient.js` (`fetchMideastControl(moduleId, day)` with `err.status`, 15-min store keyed `module:day` + payload day, shared in-flight promise, errors not cached); `src/ukraineControlLayer.js` parametrised IN PLACE — `createUkraineControlLayer({ config = UKRAINE_CONTROL_CONFIG, fetchControl, layerId, pickKey, i18nPrefix, staleDays, rasterBbox })`, `paintControlCanvas(…, { fills:[{code,css,alpha}], codes, contestedCss })`, `nearestSide(points, lon, lat, maxKm, sides)`, `setRasterBbox`, alias `createWikiControlLayer`; the regex-pinned `_raster = controlRaster(…)` line is kept for the Ukraine config, other configs go through `wikiControlRaster`; `src/mideastControlLayer.js` `createMideastControl({ viewer })` = one lazily created layer per module (`mideast-control:<id>`, pick key `mideastControl:<id>`, DataSource/entity ids per instance so hover handlers never steal each other's picks), raster bbox = theatre rect + 0.2°, API `setTheatre/setEnabled/isEnabled/show/hide/isShown/setStyle/setDay/getState/onChange/destroy`, inert without a viewer; the zone raster is drawn only for the FIRST module of a theatre's `control` list (`setZonesVisible(false)` on the others — south-lebanon = IP zones + Lebanese points), the manager remembers the day each layer was filled for and reloads on `show()`/`sync()` when `setDay` changed it meanwhile; point culling `controlPointRelevance(p, raster, { cells, contested, outside })` takes the module's contested code (IP 10, not Ukraine's 3 = Hamas) and treats points outside the theatre raster as 'rear' (Ukraine defaults unchanged); `fetchMideastControl` rejects a 200 without a `points` array as `bad_control_payload`. **Panel:** `createMideastPanel({ control })` adds the KONTROLA SÍDIEL chip (`.mideast-chip-control`, `aria-pressed`, disabled when the theatre has no modules) and a legend rebuilt on `control.onChange` PER MODULE (title, inline colour swatches + label + count for sides with points, contested/mixed, „stav k <date> · Wikipédia · CC BY-SA 4.0 · <age>" with an amber ZASTARANÉ badge over the module's staleDays — the full attribution „Wikipedia contributors · Module:<title> · CC BY-SA 4.0" (config `attribution` = the archive's snapshot string) only as that line's title tooltip; the point hover tip carries settlement · side · kind, no attribution — loading/missing/error states (a shown module with no snapshot, no error and not loading renders 'missing', never an endless 'loading'), one „odvodené" note) — never one merged legend, because the same icon file means a different side in each module. i18n `mideast.part.control`, `mideast.ctl.*` (loading/missing/error/derived/since/source/contested/mixed/none/pressure + 12 kinds; `pressure` is the declension-free „pod tlakom zo strany {side} · smer {dir}", looked up through the same prefix chain as the side texts) and `mideast.ctl.<module>.title/.<side>` for all four modules in BOTH dictionaries; SK „kontestované" for a contested settlement in BOTH modules — the Middle East module adopts the established UKRAJINA term and does not change UKRAJINA strings (a draft that switched both to „sporné" was reverted before commit: it was an agent's taste call, not the owner's). **main.js:** the manager is created BEFORE the panel and passed as `control`, `window.__godsEyeView.mideastControl`; `runMideastTheatre` calls `setTheatre(scene)` after the scene state and before framing; `runFrontScene`, `runChokepointScene` and the general `frameConflict` branch call `setTheatre(null)`; the KARTA style is forwarded like `applyUkraineZoneStyle` (hillshade → 'karta'); `revealGate.onChange` calls `mideastControl.show()/hide()` (points and raster vanish with the planet view, the chip state is untouched); deliberately NOT chained into `ukraineBase.setSideResolver`. **Review findings applied** (parallel fix agent): `plainLabel` strips HTML tags (Syrian bilingual labels), `Location dot dark red` folded onto `darkred`, the Yemen 1-px grey SAUDI ARABIA label carrier is skipped only at marksize ≤ 1, proxy `stopped` flag, gzip q-value + `Vary`, `controlFor` walk-back, `now` validation, streaming size cap. **Client review applied** (17 findings, 2026-09-26): module contested code + outside-raster 'rear' in culling, zones only for the first module of a two-module theatre, reveal gate wiring, `bad_control_payload`, per-layer day tracking, `mideast.ctl.pressure`, config `attribution` aligned to the archive string (the wikiControl test regex follows), SK labels (Yemen government „medzinárodne uznaná vláda a koalícia vedená Saudskou Arábiou", „Ansarulláh (húsíovia) a spojenci", AQAP/SDF/ISIL expanded, „miestni obyvatelia (Libanon)", „kontestované" in both modules as in UKRAJINA), doc fixes (per-side colours, Syria head 622 merged, the real attribution UI). **Pitfalls:** in the Browser pane the 3D tiles return 403 and `camera.flyTo` never settles → verify with `setView`; the same icon = a different side per module (`Dot green 0d0` = PA in IP but Houthis in Yemen; red = Hezbollah / Yemen government / Russia; grey 68a = LAF / AQAP / Syrian government) — legends per module, never shared; the Syria module uses UNQUOTED Lua values and `mark=` without a space; a raster over a whole module bbox is huge (Syria 7 719 points over 6.8°×5.1°) → per-theatre bbox only; `\b` in a bash heredoc becomes 0x08 and passes `node --check` → regex-heavy files only through Edit/Write. Tests: wikiControl 17, mideastArchive 9, mideastEventsProxy 8, mideastControlClient 3, mideastControlLayer 10 (incl. main.js tripwires with the reveal gate, culling, two-module zones, setDay-while-hidden), mideastPanel 11; full suite green under Node 24 except the 3 known worldOverlayAllocation reds. - **MIDDLE EAST module, stage 5a — STRAIT TRANSITS from IMF PortWatch** (2026-09-26, owner: „pokračuj"; plan ch. 6 stage 5 pulled forward as the quick win; checklist of the `new-data-layer` skill answered in chat: public ArcGIS, no key, IMF reuse terms with attribution, 4 × 2 820 rows server-side and 4 × 400 numbers to the browser, ESTIMATES from AIS). **Model** `src/data/portwatch.js` (pure): catalogue of four chokepoints (`hormuz`=chokepoint6, `bab-el-mandeb`=4, `suez`=1, `cape`=7 — the Cape of Good Hope is the Red Sea detour), each with a NAMED pre-crisis window (Hormuz: the year before the Iran war 2025-02-28 – 2026-02-27; the other three: before the Houthi attacks 2023-01-01 – 2023-11-15), `portwatchQueryUrl` (portid validated by regex and the day by `isDay` — nothing user-controlled reaches the SQL `where`), `parsePortwatchFeatures` → compact rows `[day, total, tanker, container, dryBulk, generalCargo, roro, capacity, capacityTanker]`, `mergePortwatchRows` (newer wins — the IMF revises preliminary days), `meanOver`, `portwatchSummary` (last day, Ø 7 / 30 days ending on the last day, % of the 7-day mean against the window mean, age, stale > 14 days, one-year spark), `portwatchKeyForTheatre` (hormuz/gulf → hormuz, red-sea/yemen → bab-el-mandeb), `fetchPortwatch` client. **Archive** `scripts/lib/mideastArchive.mjs` `portwatchRefresh` (first run = whole series from 2019 in pages of 1 000; later from last day − 45 days; 6 h freshness from `fetchedAt`; stale keeps the old file; never throws) → `.gev-cache/mideast/events/portwatch/<key>.json`, `portwatchPayload` (tail of `days` rows + the window mean from the FULL series). **Proxy** `/api/mideast/events/portwatch?keys=&days=` (allowlist → 400 bad_keys, days clamped 30–1 000, 404 no_portwatch_snapshot, cache 10 min) and archiver job `portwatch` (every 6 h, first 440 s after start, 1.5 s between chokepoints — the proxy test now expects five timers). **Card** `src/portwatchCard.js` in a new panel section PRECHODY ÚŽINAMI · MMF PORTWATCH (`mideastPanel.transitsMount`, between the control legend and the news): per chokepoint the name, Ø 7 days, the % badge (down red, up amber — a busier detour is not good news), a 168 × 28 sparkline of the year with the window mean dashed, „posledný deň … · počet lodí: N · tankery Ø 7 dní" (phrased without numeral agreement — „1 lodí" was wrong) and the named window; footer „stav k <last day> · age · ZASTARANÉ > 14 d · MMF PortWatch ↗" with the IMF attribution as the link tooltip; honest note „odhady MMF z AIS, predbežné … porovnanie počíta OKO (odvodené)". Lazy like the bulletin: first download only when the panel is expanded, refresh every 30 min while open; `setActive` highlights the chokepoint of the active theatre (`runMideastTheatre`) or chokepoint scene (`runChokepointScene`: hormuz / bab-el-mandeb / suez), cleared by front scenes and the generic frame. - **MIDDLE EAST module, stage 3 — news per theatre, hot cards on the place struck** (2026-09-26, owner: „pokračuj"; plan ch. 6 stage 3; 14 candidate feeds probed the same day against draft theatre filters). **Regions:** `SITUATION_REGIONS` gains `iran`, `lebanon`, `palestine`, `israel`, `redsea`, `syria`, `iraq` — each a GDELT query `(places) (action words) sourcelang:english`, a simple Google News fallback (`googleLimit` 15), a title filter `match` and its own direct feeds from the shared `MIDEAST_FEEDS` (BBC, Al Jazeera, Asharq Al-Awsat, The National, Al-Monitor, UN News, The Times of Israel, Haaretz, NNA, Iran International, France 24, The Guardian; limits 5–8 per outlet). Headline + link only: `unfurl:false` everywhere except BBC, the Guardian keeps its feed thumbnail (`feedImage`); Al Jazeera lost the og:image unfurl in `gulf`/`mideast` too (its T&C forbid scraping — the UKRAJINA rule); badges `official-lb` (NNA, state agency), `un` (UN News), `exile` (Iran International). „Saudi/Riyadh" is NOT in the Yemen filter (it caught domestic Saudi news: 59 of Asharq's 300 items). `mideast` also reads Asharq, Guardian and France 24. The situation proxy downloads a feed once per 5 min for all regions (`fetchFeedText` memo in `vite.config.js`; a failed download is not remembered). **Theatres:** `newsRegion` is now overview→mideast, hormuz/gulf→gulf, iran→iran, south-lebanon→lebanon, gaza/west-bank→palestine, israel→israel, red-sea/yemen→redsea, south-syria→syria, iraq→iraq; `MIDEAST_BULLETIN_REGIONS` (mideastTheatres.js) = nine chips in the panel's bulletin (default „Blízky východ", then Záliv and the theatres; the row wraps), picking a theatre flips the chip, a chip reads another theatre's news without moving the camera. **Placing a card — actor vs. target** (`locateIncident` for the Middle East gazetteer, `src/data/gulfIncidents.js`): whole words (Hama ≠ Hamas, Aden ≠ Laden), the longest name wins over a name inside it (Gulf of Aden ≠ Aden), every mention scored — tier of the place (named place > area/sea > country) then its role: a locative („in/at/near/on/over/across … X") +30, a struck object („strikes/hits/killed X") +20, „from X" −30, an actor („X strikes/says/fires…", „X's army…", „… by X", passive „hit by" excluded) −2500 (below every other mention), a demonym/organisation −5, and a demonym before a weapon or force („Israeli drone…", „in Israeli strike") is not a place at all unless a facility follows („Iranian military base"). „Israel strikes Iran" → Iran, „Iran fires missiles at Israel" → Israel, „Israel strikes Houthi targets" → Yemen, „Tehran says it struck Israeli bases" → Israel. `kind` (`area`/`sea`/`country`) and `broad` on gazetteer entries; a broad match (countries, wide seas, West Bank, Bekaa, Galilee, Negev) comes back `approx: true` and `buildIncidents` now passes `loc.approx` on, so the card says „(približne)". Gazetteer +~95 places (south Lebanon villages, Gaza and West Bank towns, Israeli towns and Nevatim, Yemen/Saudi infrastructure, Syria, Iraq, Iran nuclear sites, Qeshm/Jask), areas Gaza (was a point), West Bank, South Lebanon, Litani, Bekaa, Golan, Jordan Valley, Galilee, Negev, countries Syria, Jordan, Saudi Arabia, UAE, Oman. `REGION_DEFAULT` of the seven theatre regions is `null` — no named place, no card (the UKRAJINA rule). **Classifier:** plurals count (`strikes`, `missiles`, `drones`, `rockets`, `explosions` were silently ignored before), a labour strike, „strike a deal" and the retrospective „October 7 attack" are removed first (anniversary pieces are not today's incident; a new attack ON the anniversary still counts), „blocked/closed/shut" is a blockade only next to a route, port, crossing, airspace or sea („CNN blocked from trip" and „SIM cards blocked" were blockade cards). **RSS titles:** `decodeRssText` (vite.config.js, shared with the cockpit brief) now decodes numeric and common named entities — The Times of Israel and Haaretz double-encode quotes („&#8216;", „&#x27;" were shown as text). **Hot cards race** (`gulfIncidentCards.js`): one in-flight request per region and a ticket — switching theatre during a load no longer draws the previous region's cards (a single shared `inFlight` used to hand region B the answer for A), `clear()` cancels a pending draw. Live probe on 8 feeds: „Israeli drone drops sound bomb over Hadatha in south Lebanon" → South Lebanon, „Two tankers hit … in Strait of Hormuz" → Hormuz, „Houthi strike on Riyadh" → Riyadh, an NNA report naming only „Israeli artillery … Israeli drone" → no card. Tests: gulfIncidents +6 (actor/target, whole words, approx, no default, classifier), situationNews +3 (queries, filters keep/drop neighbours, feeds: https, no duplicates, limits ≤ 8, image rules, badges, none on the EU blocklist), `src/gulfIncidentCards.test.mjs` 3 (new — late answer, clear, failed load), conflictBulletin +2, mideastTheatres +1 and the newsRegion map, main.js tripwire. DATA_SOURCES: BBC and Al Jazeera rows updated, one row for the ten new publisher feeds with their terms (personal, non-commercial; Times of Israel allows links with the headline; Iran International and NNA publish no reuse terms).
- **Public access through a Cloudflare Tunnel, noindex everywhere** (2026-09-13, user: „daj mi to zatiaľ pod doménu uhrin.digital cez CF tunel, ale SEO noindex"). The dev server keeps binding to localhost only (the CLAUDE.md rule stands): `cloudflared` on this machine — already serving the user's other tunnels — forwards `https://oko.uhrin.digital/` to `http://localhost:4173`. Setup script `scripts/oko-tunnel-setup.ps1` (idempotent, no secrets): named tunnel `oko` (id `b1f2ad76-c2a8-4981-88b2-80819a01d979`, credentials JSON in `~/.cloudflared/`, never read by the agent), `~/.cloudflared/config-oko.yml` (ingress hostname → localhost:4173, catch-all 404, log `oko.log`), Scheduled Task „OKO Cloudflare Tunnel" (at logon, current user, auto-restart, like the „OKO dev server" watchdog). App side: `server.allowedHosts` gained `.uhrin.digital` (any other Host still gets 403), `noIndexPlugin()` is the first Vite plugin (dev and preview) and puts `X-Robots-Tag: noindex, nofollow, noarchive` on every response and serves `/robots.txt` = `Disallow: /`, `index.html` carries `<meta name="robots" content="noindex, nofollow, noarchive">`; tripwires in `src/noIndex.test.mjs`. Lesson: `~/.cloudflared/cert.pem` is bound to ONE zone chosen at `cloudflared tunnel login` (here palmshub.net), so `cloudflared tunnel route dns oko oko.uhrin.digital` silently created `oko.uhrin.digital.palmshub.net` → the script now only prints the CNAME to create (`oko` → `<tunnel-id>.cfargotunnel.com`, proxied) unless `-RouteDns -OriginCert <zone cert>` is given. Done afterwards with the user logged into the Cloudflare dashboard in the Browser pane (the agent clicked, never saw a secret): CNAME `oko` → `<tunnel-id>.cfargotunnel.com` (proxied) in the `uhrin.digital` zone, the stray `oko.uhrin.digital.palmshub.net` record deleted, and — briefly — a Zero Trust Access self-hosted application `oko` with an e-mail one-time-PIN policy. The user wanted the address open („chcel som plný prístup") and the OpenAI voice key is not configured on this machine, so the Access application was deleted again the same evening; the lesson is recorded (ask before adding a login gate the user did not ask for). End-to-end after that: `https://oko.uhrin.digital/`, `/robots.txt` and `/api/gas/status` answer 200 through Cloudflare (CF-RAY VIE) with `X-Robots-Tag`, no redirect. What still bounds abuse of the open URL: noindex everywhere, the Google key's referrer + Map Tiles-only restriction, the Map Tiles daily quota and billing alert, rate limiters in the proxies, and the fact that GIE/ENTSOG/AISStream feeds are free.

A browser that already chose the old tile keeps `local-energy` ON in its stored state until the row is switched off once.
- **The public address now serves a production build; the dev server keeps only `/api`** (2026-09-14, user: „veľmi pomaly to načíta"). Measured through the tunnel: the dev server pushed the unbundled Cesium dep (10.3 MB, 2.0 MB zstd, 1.5 s), `ui.js` (0.5 MB zstd) and hundreds of `/src/*.js` modules at ~100 ms each, all over the home uplink. Now `vite build` (12 s, `dist/` 38 MB incl. the copied Cesium assets; index chunk 620 kB gzip, regions 684 kB, egm96 geoid 1.85 MB, marine 223 kB) is served by `scripts/oko-static-server.mjs` — a dependency-free Node server on 127.0.0.1:4174 (hashed `/assets/*` immutable for a year so Cloudflare keeps them at the edge: second fetch of the index chunk was an edge HIT in 0.14 s; `index.html` `no-cache` so a new build shows at once; `X-Robots-Tag` and `/robots.txt` like the dev plugin; no traversal, no listing) — and `~/.cloudflared/config-oko.yml` routes `^/api(/.*)?$` to the dev server on 4173 and everything else to 4174. One process keeps the live feeds (AISStream, OpenSky, the SQLite recorder): `vite preview` was rejected because only 14 of 28 proxy plugins have a preview hook and a second full server would double every feed. `scripts/oko-publish.ps1` = build → (re)register and restart the Scheduled Task „OKO public static" → rewrite the ingress → restart the tunnel task; `-SkipBuild` reuses `dist/`. Publishing is therefore a deliberate step: localhost:4173 stays live-reloading, the domain shows the last published build. Tests: `src/staticServer.test.mjs` spawns the server on a free port (index/asset headers, ETag/304, robots, 404, traversal, `/api` → 502, HEAD, 405). Google Maps key (project oko-dev, „Maps Platform API Key", API restriction Map Tiles API only — confirmed on a fresh load before saving, so the console's „usage detected for geocoding / street view" warning concerned calls that were already blocked): website restriction `https://oko.uhrin.digital/*` added next to `http://localhost:4173/*` with the user logged into Google Cloud Console in the pane (the key value was never shown). Not checked: Cesium ion token „allowed URLs" (the tokens page prints token values, so the agent stays away from it — if ion tiles fail on the public host while they work locally, that is the place). A second stray record `nextcloud.uhrin.digital.palmshub.net` (older, not ours) sits in palmshub.net. Route `/api/gas/lng-fleet` in `gasProxy()` reads `_aisStreamVessels` of this dev-server session and **never** calls `ensureAisStreamConnection()` — the feed is started (and paid for in bandwidth) only by the Live AIS Vessels layer; without it the answer says `feed.active:false` and the card shows „feed AIS nebeží" with a ZAPNÚŤ AIS button (`onEnableAis` in `ui.js`, reload after 8 s). Payload: prefiltered rows (mmsi, name, IMO, confidence + reason, Wikidata ship record, length, draught, speed, course, nav status, destination, ETA, position, fresh/last-known), counts (scanned, lng, confirmed, likely), feed state, list provenance; `no-store`. Card `fleet` after LNG in `src/gasPanel.js`: headline count, „potvrdené n · pravdepodobné n · smerujú na terminály EÚ n · v pohybe n", rows sorted EU-bound → confirmed → fresh (flag from MMSI via `mmsiFlag`, confidence, „→ EÚ", length · built · speed · destination · ETA · age; reason and operator as the note), OBNOVIŤ button, own 10-minute timer (`LNG_FLEET_REFRESH_MS`), row click = `onFlyToVessel` in `ui.js` (enables the ships layer, `selectById(mmsi)` now and after 6 s, camera 40 km above). Tests: `lngFleet.test.mjs` (index, tanker type, classifier incl. negatives — „GAS" alone is LPG, Höegh car carrier, BW under 250 m —, payload, feed states, model sort and texts, fetch, list integrity: ≥ 200 ships, 7-digit unique IMO, CC0 in SOURCE.md), panel test (card, fly-to, refresh, off state with the enable button, error path, tripwires: route present and free of `ensureAisStreamConnection`, `no-store`, ui hooks, CSS, i18n).
- **Public-address console clean-up** (2026-09-14, user pasted the DevTools console of oko.uhrin.digital: `GET /assets/local_data/flags/4x3/tr.svg 404`, `POST /api/openai/hud-summary 503` every 15 s, `WebSocket wss://oko.uhrin.digital/ failed`, sporadic `502`, Google 3D `403`). Four causes, three fixes. **Flags** — `countryFlags.js` built the flag directory from `import.meta.url`, which in the bundle is `/assets/index-<hash>.js`, so the build pointed at `/assets/local_data/flags/…` that `vite build` never emits; the SVGs now live in **`public/flags/4x3/`** (Vite public dir: served verbatim in dev and copied into `dist/`), `flagUrl()` returns `${BASE_URL}flags/4x3/<iso2>.svg`, `scripts/fetch-flags.mjs` writes them there (tables + provenance stay in `src/data/local_data/flags/`). **HUD summary** — `src/hudSummaryPolicy.js` (`classifySummaryFailure`): a 503 whose body says `OPENAI_API_KEY is not set`, or a 401/403, marks the AI summary off for the session (`hud._summaryDisabled`, one `console.info`), the composed local summary stays; 502/5xx/429 keep retrying as before. **Voice hint** — `fetchRealtimeToken` tags the thrown error with `code` (`classifyTokenFailure`: 503 + "not set" → `voice-unconfigured`, 403 → `voice-forbidden`), `createErrorRecord` keeps it, and `reportError` swaps the static "check microphone and network" line for `voice.error-hint-unconfigured` / `-forbidden` (`voiceErrorHintKey`). **502s** — cloudflared's log showed `wsarecv: An existing connection was forcibly closed` on 4173 in bursts (six at one second), never on 4174: Node closes idle keep-alive sockets after 5 s while cloudflared pools them for 90 s, and when the dev server's event loop is blocked for a moment the timer fires exactly as the next request lands → RST → 502; `originKeepAlivePlugin` (vite.config.js) and the static server now hold idle connections 120 s (`headersTimeout` 125 s), i.e. longer than the proxy. **The main cause of the slow `/api` was outside the code:** every Task Scheduler task runs at priority 7 = **BelowNormal** by default, so on a busy machine (Docker terrain tiling, Edge with Cesium, the Hermes agent, Defender) the dev server got almost no CPU — `/robots.txt` took 3–10 s or timed out, `/api/*` 80–100 s, the tunnel answered 502; switching the running dev server, static server and cloudflared to Normal (`PriorityClass`) dropped `/robots.txt` to 2–5 ms at once. The three tasks now have `Settings.Priority = 4` and `install-oko-server-task.ps1`, `oko-publish.ps1`, `oko-tunnel-setup.ps1` register with `-Priority 4` (tripwire `src/schedulerTasks.test.mjs`, which also pins the UTF-8 BOM of those scripts). **Second cause, in the code:** `GET /api/history/status` ran `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM fixes` over 25 M rows synchronously (`node:sqlite`) — 42 s during which the whole dev server stood still (`/robots.txt` took 30 s); `flightHistoryStore.js` now keeps `fixes_count` / `legs_count` in a `meta` table updated inside the same transactions as the inserts and the pruning (the full COUNT runs once at the first open without `meta`, or on demand via `store.recount()`), MIN/MAX(t) stay on the `fixes_t` index, so `status()` is O(1). **Not bugs:** the `wss://oko.uhrin.digital/` failures are Vite's HMR client in a tab that loaded the page *before* the switch to the static build (the build has no WebSocket at all — verified: no `ws` entries in the tunnel log after a fresh load); the Google 3D Tiles `403` is the known EEA block with the Cesium ion fallback taking over (see Fáza 0).
- **Photorealistic tiles: `dynamicScreenSpaceError` off** (2026-09-15, user with an oblique low view of Bratislava castle: „prečo mi nevykreslí dobre google mapu?" — a torn, scrambled band across mid-screen). CesiumJS enables `dynamicScreenSpaceError` by default on 3D tilesets; it inflates the allowed screen-space error for mid/far tiles to save load, and at a near-ground oblique angle (~450 m altitude, −28° pitch) it selects coarse ancestor tiles whose simplified mesh renders as spiky torn geometry. Diagnosed live in the pane (single ion-fallback tileset, `tilesLoaded: true`, nothing pending — so it was the *final* LOD, not a loading glitch): toggling `dynamicScreenSpaceError` off refined tiles from 76 to 133 selected and the mesh went crisp; `foveatedScreenSpaceError` left at its default `true` (it only softens screen edges, hidden by the scope mask). Fixed in `PHOTOREAL_TILESET_OPTIONS` (`src/photorealTileset.js`), so both the Google and the EEA ion-fallback path get it; tripwire in `photorealTileset.test.mjs`. Verified on the freshly published build.
- **Mobile shell — the whole app is responsive** (2026-09-14, user on a phone at work: „nevedel som sa preklikať… celý web má byť responzívny", picked option A). `src/mobileShell.js` (`initMobileShell` from `main.js`, `window.__okoMobileShell` for diagnostics) resolves a shell mode from `innerWidth`/`innerHeight` + `(pointer: coarse)`: **mobile** when the width is ≤ 900 px or a touch device is ≤ 1180 px wide (phone portrait, phone landscape, tablets), **landscape** additionally when the viewport is ≤ 520 px tall and wider than tall. In mobile mode `body.oko-mobile` hides `#left-panel-stack`, `#right-context-rail`, `#pp-toggles`, `#cctv-panel`, the Intel HUD (and its DISPLAY toggle), `#style-indicator` and the title subtitle; the title bar, `#top-center-actions` and `#lang-switch` shrink into one top row; the command dock, toast, view switcher and cockpit entry lift above a fixed bottom **app bar** `#oko-appbar` (static markup in `index.html`, Material Symbols, i18n keys `mobile.*`) with five sections — **Vrstvy** (`#data-panel`), **Scény** (`#scene-panel`), **Dáta** (`#gas-panel` + `#history-panel`), **Zobrazenie** (`#pp-toggles` + `#cctv-panel` + `#global-context-panel`) and **Hľadať** (expands the dock's location bar and focuses `#location-search`; no sheet). Tapping a section opens `#oko-sheet` (a bottom sheet, max 62 dvh, above the dock lift; in landscape a 430 px / 72 vw drawer from the right): the section's panels are **re-parented into the sheet body** (same DOM nodes, ids and listeners intact — the StyleManager rig ignores them there because they are no longer children of the stack or rail), the first panel is expanded and the others stay collapsed as tappable headers (`setPanelCollapsed(id, …, { persist: false, syncShare: false })`), and closing (× button, backdrop tap, Escape, swipe down on the sheet head ≥ 80 px or ≥ 30 px at ≥ 0.5 px/ms — `swipeShouldClose`) restores each panel before its original next sibling with its original collapsed state; one sheet at a time, the dock is hidden while a sheet is open, and switching back to desktop closes it. `.oko-in-sheet` CSS neutralises the panels' fixed positions, widths and inline max-heights and hides their tab-collapse `◀` buttons. Touch extras: `scene.pick()` is wrapped on coarse pointers so every pick box is at least 18 px (`installCoarsePickBox`, own property over Cesium's prototype — covers the Viewer's default click and the layers' handlers), the detection hover card is skipped on coarse pointers (an emulated mousemove would open the hover card and the tap card together), `#cesiumContainer { touch-action: none }` keeps pinch/drag for Cesium, the viewport meta carries `viewport-fit=cover` with `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` on the bar, and the world overlay host got `setWorldOverlayLaneSuppressed(laneId, …)`: the shell suppresses the `ambient-card` lane on mobile (custom painters and queued entries of that lane are skipped, no hit rects), so only the selected and tracked cards paint on a phone. The first-run launcher becomes a bottom list above the bar with 56 px tiles. Follow-up the same night (user at 827 px: „toto sa prekrýva"): in mobile mode the dock's tray labels are icon-only (the „Vizuálne predvoľby" title was 160 px wide inside a 68 px tray and ran under the voice pill) and the shell lifts `#cesium-credits` inline to `lift + dock height + 8 px` (136 px by default) so the Cesium/Google credit line sits above the dock instead of in its band — both elements are modelled by `creditAttribution.test.mjs`, which is why the lifts are inline styles rather than CSS rules. Verified live in the Browser pane at 375×812 (bottom sheet: LAYERS, DISPLAY), 844×390 (right drawer: SCENES, search), 768×1024 (tablet sheet) and 1440×900 (desktop untouched: no body class, bar hidden, stack/rail/HUD displayed). Tests: `src/mobileShell.test.mjs` (mode table, swipe thresholds, pick box wrapper, re-parenting round-trips with a fake DOM, one sheet at a time, desktop switch closes, inert without markup) and `src/overlays/worldOverlayLaneSuppression.test.mjs`. Known limits: the Cockpit view keeps its own layout; the HUD is simply off on mobile (no compact variant yet); panels inside the sheet keep their desktop typography.
- **First-run launcher is opt-in now** (2026-09-14, user on the public address circled the „Vyber si svoj prvý pohľad" card: „toto úplne zruš alebo to daj hidden, aby sa to pri načítaní neobjavovalo — neviem, načo je to"). `FIRST_RUN_AUTO_SHOW = false` in `src/firstRunExperience.js` makes `shouldShowFirstRun()` return false unless the URL carries `?welcome=1` (the demo/support replay still works, a share link still outranks it); `initFirstRunExperience` therefore removes the `#first-run-launcher` node on every ordinary load. The module, its missions (`FIRST_RUN_MISSIONS`, incl. `sk-overview`) and the tool-schema pin stay untouched for the replay path. A fresh visitor now starts with the layer registry defaults, not with a mission.
- **Sharing with a picture, also for social networks (A+B)** (2026-09-14, user: „pri zdieľaní hocijakej časti systému sa musí objaviť aktuálne image presne toho, čo zdieľam… aj na soc. siete", picked A+B). Three layers. **Subject in the link** — `src/shareSubject.js`: the hash carries `subj=<layerId>.<kind>.<id>` (`t` = tracked via `trackById`: `flights`, `military`, `satellites`; `s` = selected via `selectById`: `ais-live-vessels`, `gas-flows`, `earthquakes`), read from `getTrackedSubject()` / `getTrackedInfo()` first and the context store's selected record second (`subjectIdFromContextId` strips the `ais-` / `gas-flows:` prefixes), written by `ShareLinkManager.setSubjectStateProvider` (ui.js also refreshes on `gev:entity-selected` / `gev:entity-selection-cleared`; tracking moves the camera, which already rewrites the hash) and restored by `ui._restoreShareSubject` after `onRestore`: `applyShareSubject` enables the layer if needed and retries every 1.5 s for 30 s while the live layer has no such object, giving up as soon as the user selects something else; `gasFlowsLayer` gained `selectById(key)` / `getSelectedKey()`. **Snapshot + panel (A)** — the share button now opens `src/sharePanel.js` (DOM built without innerHTML, so it is unit-tested with a fake document): `ShareLinkManager.buildShareUrl()` (long link with `at`), `captureShareSnapshot` (`src/shareSnapshot.js`: `renderFreshCesiumFrame`, the Cesium canvas plus every `#world-overlay-root canvas` composited into 1200×630 with a centred cover crop, a bottom strip with `OKO · <date>` and `buildAttributionLine(#cesium-credits text)` = „© Google · Cesium ion …" because the Map Tiles terms require attribution on shared images, JPEG re-encoded down to ≤ 400 kB), then `publishShareSnapshot` → `POST /api/share`; the panel shows the preview, the short link (or the long one with a toast when the server is unreachable), Copy link, Copy picture (`ClipboardItem` PNG), Share… (Web Share with the PNG as a file — Instagram/TikTok/stories), Save picture, and eight network intents from `buildShareTargets` (Facebook, X, LinkedIn, Threads, Bluesky, WhatsApp, Telegram, e-mail); title/description come from `buildShareCopy` (subject label, `#location-mini-city`, enabled layer names, time). i18n `share.*`. **Server (B)** — `src/shareStore.js` + `sharePlugin()` in vite.config.js: `POST /api/share` validates the payload (hash ≤ 4 096 chars of the URLSearchParams alphabet with `lat=`, JPEG magic bytes, ≤ 400 kB, 200–4096 px, texts trimmed of control chars), rate-limits 30/h per IP and 300/h globally keyed by `CF-Connecting-IP` behind the tunnel (the socket is always `::1` there), stores `<id>.json` + `<id>.jpg` under `.gev-cache/share` (junction → D:) with a random 10-char base62 id and 90-day retention (pruned on save, once an hour), and answers `{ id, url, image }` with the origin derived from `Host` + `X-Forwarded-Proto`; `GET /s/<id>` renders `renderSharePage`: Open Graph + Twitter `summary_large_image` tags with absolute image URL, `<meta name="robots" content="noindex">`, a meta-refresh and `location.replace` into `/#<hash>`; `GET /s/<id>.jpg` is immutable for a year. Because social crawlers must be able to fetch the share pages, both robots.txt files (dev `noIndexPlugin`, `scripts/oko-static-server.mjs`) now read `Disallow: /` + `Allow: /s/`, the `X-Robots-Tag` header is skipped for `/s/<id>` and the tunnel ingress in `scripts/oko-publish.ps1` routes `^/(api|s)(/.*)?$` to the dev server. Verified live on localhost: the panel showed the actual 3D view with the strip, a short link, eight networks; `/s/<id>` served the OG tags and the JPEG. Tests: `shareSubject`, `sharelink.subject`, `shareTargets`, `sharePanel`, `shareStore`, `shareServer` (tripwires), updated `noIndex` / `staticServer` / `gasFlowsLayer`. **First real Facebook post (same evening) showed only the bare domain** — three follow-ups: (1) the share page no longer carries `<meta http-equiv="refresh">` — Facebook's scraper follows meta refresh (unlike JavaScript) and landed on the root without the hash and without OG tags; the redirect is `location.replace` only, with a plain link for no-JS readers; (2) the root page got default Open Graph / Twitter tags with `public/share-default.jpg` (1200×630, generated by `scripts/build-share-default-image.mjs` with sharp: logo, OKO, SK+EN subtitle, domain), so a pasted root URL or a long hash link still gets a card; (3) robots.txt on both servers is now `Disallow: /api/` + `Allow: /` — the earlier `Disallow: /` hid the pages from the social crawlers (X and LinkedIn honour robots.txt) and would have let Google list the URL without content anyway; "no index" is carried by the `noindex` meta and the `X-Robots-Tag` header (still skipped only on `/s/<id>`). The panel also got a highlighted warning note plus a „Skúsiť znova" button whenever only the long link is available, console warnings for a failed snapshot/upload, and the server logs each stored share.
- **`img.decode()` never settles in a hidden tab** (2026-09-10, found live while chasing "Historical Ship Density will not switch on"): three layers awaited `img.decode()` before handing the element on — `densityDrape.js` (ship + air density), `shmuRadar.js` (radar frames) and `meteoLayer.js` (every GFS field and every timeline step). Measured in the browser with `document.visibilityState === 'hidden'`: the PNG fetched in 7 ms, `onload` fired, `complete` was true and `naturalWidth` was 1440, yet `decode()` had still neither resolved nor rejected after 6 s — identically for a detached image and one appended to the document. Because the promise never SETTLES, the existing `.catch(() => {})` and `.then(ok, fail)` guards could not help, so `enable()` never returned and the manager left the layer in `enabling` forever; the maritime-context panel, which awaits all three of its layers, stayed frozen with both buttons disabled. Anyone who switches browser tabs while a layer loads hits this, and it does not clear on return. Fix: new `src/data/imageDecode.js` with `awaitImageDecode(img)` — `decode()` may delay the result but can never decide it. It is skipped entirely when `document.hidden` (it provably will not run there, and meteo loads an image per forecast step), raced against `DECODE_BUDGET_MS` = 1 500 ms otherwise, and always resolves, never rejects; `onload` plus `naturalWidth > 0` already guarantees a usable element for Cesium's texture upload. All three call sites now go through it, pinned by a tripwire that forbids a bare `img.decode().then/catch` in those files. Verified live in a hidden tab: the density layer reaches `enabled`. Not covered here: the manager has no lifecycle timeout, so a future layer that hangs in `enable()` will still wedge silently.
- **Ships: hover card + hover bracket, and why the world outside Europe looks empty** (2026-09-12, user: "lode okrem Európy nevidí skoro nikde, ani keď na ne prejdem myšou tak nič, a zameriavače ako u lietadiel"). Three separate things. (1) **Hover card** — `installContactHoverCard` resolves `module.getContactSummary(sourceId)` for every candidate and `hoverCandidatesFromPick` already emitted `{ layerId: 'ais-live-vessels', sourceId: mmsi }`, but the vessel module never implemented `getContactSummary`, so a ship pick resolved to nothing and the card stayed hidden. `aisLiveVessels.getContactSummary(mmsi)` now returns the flights-shaped summary the shared `hoverCardModel` renders without a vessel branch: ship name as headline, speed (knots × 0.514444 → m/s for the shared formatter) · course (true heading preferred, AIS sentinels 511/360 dropped), `UNDER WAY · CARGO · 121 m · PBXY` as the machine line (nav status in `operator`, normalised type + length in `type`, call sign in `registration`; `type` is never empty so the card never falls back to `t('aircraft.category.…')`), `→ ROTTERDAM` as route text, flag from the MMSI MID, `AISStream · fix age`, and last-known positions honestly flagged via `stale` ("bez fixu — odhad"). Verified live: hovering CALYPSO shows the card. (2) **Hover bracket** — detection asks non-flight layers for at most `LAYER_CANDIDATE_CAP` = 2 600 objects by deterministic stride; with ~33 000 ships on screen that is one in thirteen, so the hovered ship was usually not in the cohort and `_isHoveredObject` had nothing to light (flights never hit this: they get `maxCount` Infinity). `_collectDetectableObjects` now passes `hovered: _hoverCandidates` and the vessel `getDetectableObjects` force-includes hovered and selected vessels on top of the stride sample (`maxCount` budgets the sample only); tripwire in `detectionHover.test.mjs`. Verified: the hovered ship is in the forced cohort and absent from the plain one.
